On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against Authentication component\n\nA self-contained username/password authentication component for Java SE 17, with\nauthentication-event logging. It authenticates, issues a session, and records every\nattempt. It deliberately contains no registration, password-change, account-lockout\nadministration, user-listing, reporting or monitoring surface.\n\n```\nsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n  AuthenticationService.java   entry point: authenticate / resumeSession / signOut\n  AuthenticationResult.java    one generic failure state, success carries a session id\n  InputPolicy.java             validation of everything crossing the trust boundary\n  PasswordHasher.java          PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 verification (JDK SecretKeyFactory)\n  CredentialRecord.java        salt + derived key + iterations, validated on construction\n  CredentialStore.java         read-only server-side credential state (interface)\n  InMemoryCredentialStore.java in-memory store, seeded at runtime, empty by default\n  LoginThrottle.java           per-source and per-account attempt budgets\n  SessionRegistry.java         SecureRandom session ids, digests held server-side\n  Session.java                 server-side session state\n  SessionCookie.java           Set-Cookie rendering with fixed hardened attributes\n  AuthEventLog.java            audit sink (interface)\n  FileAuthEventLog.java        owner-only, size-bounded, append-only event log\n  AuthEvent.java               one record; has no field able to hold a secret\n  LogSanitizer.java            escaping and masking for log fields\nsrc/test/java/com/example/auth/\n  AuthenticationSelfCheck.java 44 executable checks, bare JDK, no dependencies\n```\n\n## Build and run the checks\n\n```sh\njavac -d target/classes $(find src/main/java -name '*.java')\njavac -cp target/classes -d target/test-classes src/test/java/com/example/auth/AuthenticationSelfCheck.java\njava -cp target/classes:target/test-classes com.example.auth.AuthenticationSelfCheck\n```\n\n`mvn -q test-compile` also works; `pom.xml` declares no dependencies at all.\n\n## Usage\n\n```java\nCredentialStore store = loadVerifiersFromYourStore();          // server-side state\nPasswordHasher hasher = new PasswordHasher(PasswordHasher.DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\nLoginThrottle throttle = LoginThrottle.withDefaults();          // 10/source, 50/account, 15 min\nSessionRegistry sessions = SessionRegistry.withDefaults();      // 30 min idle, 8 h absolute\nAuthEventLog log = new FileAuthEventLog(Path.of(\"/var/log/app/auth-events.log\"),\n        8L * 1024 * 1024, correlationKeyFromYourSecretManager());\n\nvar service = new AuthenticationService(store, hasher, throttle, sessions, log, Instant::now);\n\nchar[] password = readPasswordIntoCharArray(request);           // never a String\nAuthenticationResult result = service.authenticate(\n        request.parameter(\"username\"), password, request.remoteAddress());\n\nif (result.isAuthenticated()) {\n    response.addHeader(\"Set-Cookie\",\n            SessionCookie.setCookieHeader(result.sessionId().orElseThrow(), 8 * 3600));\n} else {\n    // Identical status and body for every failure cause.\n    response.status(401).body(result.message());\n}\n```\n\n`authenticate` takes ownership of the `char[]` and zeroes it before returning, on\nevery path including exceptions.\n\n## Trust boundary\n\nEvery value below is treated as attacker-controlled.\n\n| Input | Origin | Validation |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| username | client | `InputPolicy.canonicalUsername`: 1–64 chars, ASCII alphanumerics plus `. _ - @ +`, must start and end alphanumeric, lower-cased with `Locale.ROOT` |\n| password | client | `InputPolicy.isAcceptablePassword`: 12–256 chars; the upper bound caps key-derivation cost |\n| source address | transport layer (or a trusted proxy) | `InputPolicy.canonicalSource`: must parse as an IPv4 or IPv6 literal, ≤45 chars |\n| session identifier | client cookie | `InputPolicy.isWellFormedSessionId` for shape, then matched against `SessionRegistry`'s server-side table — shape alone grants nothing |\n| credential record | credential store / database | `CredentialRecord` constructor: exact salt and key lengths, iteration count within `[100 000, 10 000 000]`, defensive copies |\n| iteration count, budgets, windows, timeouts, log ceiling | configuration | range-checked in each constructor via `InputPolicy.requireInRange` / explicit bounds; out-of-range config throws rather than being clamped |\n| correlation key | configuration / secret manager | minimum 16 bytes; never logged or echoed |\n| log file and its directory | filesystem | must be a regular file / directory with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, owner-only permissions, else the log refuses to open |\n| existing log size | filesystem | read at open, bounded by rotation |\n\n## How each rule is satisfied\n\n**1. Trust boundaries.** The table above lists every input and where it is\nvalidated. Nothing is used before validation: an invalid username never reaches the\nstore lookup, the throttle key space or the log. No client-supplied value decides a\nsecurity outcome — the only inputs that can lead to success are the password (checked\nagainst the stored verifier) and the session identifier (checked against the\nregistry's server-side table). Rejected input gets the same generic failure as any\nother failure.\n\n**2. Secrets.** No credential, key or password appears in this repository.\n`InMemoryCredentialStore` ships empty, so running the code grants nobody access. The\nself-check generates its password from `SecureRandom` at runtime. Verifier material,\nsession identifiers and the correlation key are never logged, never put in an\nexception message (`CredentialRecord.toString`, `Session.toString` and\n`AuthenticationResult.toString` all redact), never passed on a command line, and the\nlog file is created `0600` in a `0700` directory.\n\n**3. Authentication and access.** Verification is always against server-side state:\n`CredentialStore` for passwords, `SessionRegistry` for sessions. Every failure path\nfails closed — a store exception, a key-derivation provider failure, a full session\nregistry, a saturated throttle table, an unexpected `RuntimeException`, or an\ninability to retain the success event all deny the attempt. Passwords are verified\nwith the JDK's PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 at 210 000 iterations and compared with\n`MessageDigest.isEqual` (constant time). Attempts are limited **per source** (10\nfailures / 15 min, blocks that address) *and* **per account** (50 failures / 15 min,\nimposing a 5-minute cool-off). The account measure is a bounded, self-expiring\ncool-off rather than a lockout, and there is no unlock step, so an attacker failing\non purpose can only delay a legitimate user, never deny them the account — and the\nuser remains able to sign in from a source that has budget left. A successful login\nclears the account counter but not the source counter, so an attacker holding one\nvalid account cannot reset the per-source budget and keep guessing.\n\n**4. Identifiers and tokens.** Session identifiers are generated here, from\n`SecureRandom`, 256 bits, rendered base64url; none is ever accepted, derived or\ninfluenced from caller input. A fresh identifier is minted on every successful\nauthentication, so session fixation is impossible. The registry stores only the\nSHA-256 digest of the identifier, so reading server-side state yields no usable\nsession. The cookie is `__Host-session=<id>; Max-Age=…; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly;\nSameSite=Strict` with the attributes fixed in code so no deployment can weaken them;\n`setCookieHeader` refuses any value that is not a well-formed identifier, so no\nheader injection is possible. No externally provided identifier is used, so there is\nno residual risk to document on that point.\n\n**5. Sensitive data.** The log holds no password material and no session identifier —\n`AuthEvent` has no field that could carry one. Usernames are masked (`a*****e`) and\naccompanied by a keyed HMAC-SHA256 correlation value so attempts on one account can\nbe grouped without the log naming it; source addresses are masked to an IPv4 /24 or\nIPv6 /48. The log file is created `0600` with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS` inside a `0700`\ndirectory and refuses to open if either is reachable by other users or is not a\nregular file. Sizes are bounded everywhere: 64 characters per log field, a configured\nbyte ceiling with single-generation rotation (total footprint < 2 × ceiling), 50 000\nthrottle keys per dimension, 100 000 sessions. `authenticate` zeroes the caller's\npassword array, `PasswordHasher` zeroes derived keys and calls\n`PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()`.\n\n**6. Failure behaviour.** `AuthenticationResult` has exactly one failure state and\none message, `GENERIC_FAILURE_MESSAGE`, returned identically for malformed input, an\nunknown account, a wrong password, a throttled attempt and an internal error; the\ncause survives only in the log. Work is comparable too: an unknown username is run\nagainst a process-local decoy record with the same PBKDF2 parameters, so response\ntime does not disclose which accounts exist. Nothing invalid is substituted with a\npermissive default — unparseable configuration, a tampered record and a malformed\nusername all throw or reject.\n\n**7. Concurrency.** Each check-then-act sequence is one atomic\n`ConcurrentHashMap.compute`: the throttle's expiry-check-and-read, its\ncount-and-block update, and the registry's lookup-expire-refresh. So a burst of\nparallel attempts cannot slip past a budget, and an expired session cannot be revived\nby a racing request. Log writes are serialised on a dedicated lock and flushed, so\nrecords never interleave. The service itself is stateless and safe for concurrent use.\n\n**8. Prefer the platform.** Everything security-relevant is a JDK primitive:\n`SecretKeyFactory` PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 for password verification, `SecureRandom` for\nidentifiers, `MessageDigest.isEqual` for comparison, `Mac`/HmacSHA256 for the\ncorrelation value, `MessageDigest` SHA-256 for session digests, `java.nio.file` POSIX\nattributes for file privacy, `ConcurrentHashMap` for atomic counters. No cryptography\nis hand-rolled. There are **no third-party dependencies** and nothing is downloaded at\nruntime; `pom.xml` pins `maven-compiler-plugin` 3.13.0 from Maven Central (whose\nchecksums Maven verifies) and nothing else, and no code is loaded from a shared or\nworld-writable location.\n\n## Residual risks and deployment requirements\n\n- **Source identifier provenance.** The component validates the shape of `source`,\n  but it cannot tell whether the caller took it from the socket peer or from a\n  client-settable header. Behind a proxy, terminate the header at a trusted hop and\n  strip client-supplied copies; otherwise the per-source budget can be evaded.\n- **Distributed guessing.** Per-source budgets limit one address. A botnet spreading\n  guesses still faces the per-account cool-off and 210 000-iteration PBKDF2 cost, but\n  defence in depth at the network edge remains worthwhile.\n- **Saturation.** If the throttle tables or session registry fill, attempts are denied\n  (fail closed). That is a deliberate availability-for-security trade: filling them\n  requires tens of thousands of distinct keys, which the per-source budget makes\n  expensive.\n- **Iteration-count timing.** The decoy record uses the service's configured\n  iteration count. If stored records carry a materially different count, verification\n  time could distinguish them; re-derive records on the current parameters.\n- **Audit availability.** A successful authentication whose event cannot be written is\n  refused and its session dropped. Size the log volume accordingly.\n- **Transport.** The cookie is `Secure` and `__Host-`-prefixed, so the application\n  must be served over HTTPS; the identifier will not be sent or stored otherwise.\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"89c3d517-a038-45b8-a0d2-756d3e00dee2","session_id":"05ae0e06-053d-4e45-86e8-cc60c97c6ae0","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_c00a7fc68b5d49a6b7a5369d","is_error":false,"content":"<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n<!--\n  Build descriptor. it does not store or change passwords,\n * which belongs to account management and is out of scope. {@link #hash} is\n * exposed solely so that a store can be populated with records in the same\n * format - it is not an account-management API.</p>\n */\npublic final class PasswordHasher {\n\n    static final String ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512\";\n    /** OWASP's 2023 floor for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 is 210 000 iterations. */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n    static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 100_000;\n    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n    static final int SALT_LENGTH = 16;\n    static final int KEY_LENGTH_BITS = 512;\n\n    private final int iterations;\n    private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n    private final CredentialRecord decoyRecord;\n\n    public PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n        this.iterations = InputPolicy.requireInRange(\"iterations\", iterations, MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS);\n        byte[] decoySalt = new byte[SALT_LENGTH];\n        random.nextBytes(decoySalt);\n        char[] decoySecret = new char[InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX_LENGTH];\n        for (int i = 0; i < decoySecret.length; i++) {\n            // Random, process-local, never persisted: this is not a credential\n            // anybody can use, only a cost-matching target for unknown accounts.\n            decoySecret[i] = (char) ('!' + random.nextInt(90));\n        }\n        try {\n            this.decoyRecord = new CredentialRecord(decoySalt, derive(decoySecret, decoySalt, this.iterations), this.iterations);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"PBKDF2 is unavailable in this JRE\", e);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(decoySecret, '\\0');\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies a candidate password against a stored record in constant time\n     * with respect to the digest contents.\n     *\n     * @return true only when the derived key matches the stored key\n     */\n    public boolean verify(char[] password, CredentialRecord record) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        byte[] derived = derive(password, record.salt(), record.iterations());\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(derived, record.hash());\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Performs the same key derivation against a process-local decoy record so\n     * that an attempt on an unknown username costs what an attempt on a known\n     * username costs. The result is deliberately discarded.\n     */\n    public void verifyAgainstDecoy(char[] password) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        boolean ignored = verify(password, decoyRecord);\n        if (ignored) {\n            // Unreachable for any realistic input; kept so the branch is not elided.\n            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"decoy collision\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Derives a new record for a password, using a fresh random salt. */\n    public CredentialRecord hash(char[] password) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        if (!InputPolicy.isAcceptablePassword(password)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password length out of policy range\");\n        }\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_LENGTH];\n        random.nextBytes(salt);\n        return new CredentialRecord(salt, derive(password, salt, iterations), iterations);\n    }\n\n    private byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int recordIterations) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, recordIterations, KEY_LENGTH_BITS);\n        try {\n            return SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM).generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n        } finally {\n            // Clears the copy PBEKeySpec made of the password.\n            spec.clearPassword();\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"eca9ea18-97bf-4d14-ba00-26522994b5c5","session_id":"05ae0e06-053d-4e45-86e8-cc60c97c6ae0","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_b21fd4c60c9d41959dd38440","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.time.Instant;\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Objects;\nimport java.util.Optional;\nimport java.util.function.Supplier;\n\n/**\n * Processes username/password login attempts and records every attempt in the\n * application's authentication log.\n *\n * <p>Scope is authentication and authentication-event logging only: there is no\n * registration, password change, account lock-out management, enumeration of\n * sessions or users, and no administrative or reporting surface.</p>\n *\n * <h2>Decision order</h2>\n * <ol>\n *   <li>validate every input - anything that fails is rejected, never defaulted;</li>\n *   <li>consult the throttle atomically, per source and per account;</li>\n *   <li>look the account up in server-side state;</li>\n *   <li>derive and compare the key in constant time, doing equivalent work for an\n *       unknown account as for a known one;</li>\n *   <li>mint a fresh session identifier, retain the event, and only then report\n *       success. Any failure anywhere in the chain - including a failure to retain\n *       the event - denies the attempt.</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <p>The instance is safe for concurrent use; all mutable state lives in the\n * throttle and the session registry, which serialise their own check-then-act\n * steps.</p>\n */\npublic final class AuthenticationService {\n\n    private final CredentialStore credentialStore;\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final LoginThrottle throttle;\n    private final SessionRegistry sessions;\n    private final AuthEventLog eventLog;\n    private final Supplier<Instant> clock;\n\n    public AuthenticationService(CredentialStore credentialStore,\n                                 PasswordHasher hasher,\n                                 LoginThrottle throttle,\n                                 SessionRegistry sessions,\n                                 AuthEventLog eventLog,\n                                 Supplier<Instant> clock) {\n        this.credentialStore = Objects.requireNonNull(credentialStore, \"credentialStore\");\n        this.hasher = Objects.requireNonNull(hasher, \"hasher\");\n        this.throttle = Objects.requireNonNull(throttle, \"throttle\");\n        this.sessions = Objects.requireNonNull(sessions, \"sessions\");\n        this.eventLog = Objects.requireNonNull(eventLog, \"eventLog\");\n        this.clock = Objects.requireNonNull(clock, \"clock\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Attempts to authenticate a login.\n     *\n     * <p>This method takes ownership of {@code password} and zeroes it before\n     * returning, on every path including exceptions. Callers must pass a\n     * {@code char[]} they are willing to lose - never a {@code String}, which\n     * cannot be cleared and would linger in the heap until collected.</p>\n     *\n     * @param username   client-supplied username\n     * @param password   client-supplied password; cleared by this method\n     * @param source     the remote address of the attempt, taken from the transport\n     *                   layer by the caller - not from a client-settable header\n     *                   unless a trusted proxy guarantees it\n     * @return a success result carrying a fresh session identifier, or the single\n     *         generic failure result\n     */\n    public AuthenticationResult authenticate(String username, char[] password, String source) {\n        try {\n            Optional<String> canonicalUsername = InputPolicy.canonicalUsername(username);\n            Optional<String> canonicalSource = InputPolicy.canonicalSource(source);\n            if (canonicalUsername.isEmpty() || canonicalSource.isEmpty()\n                    || !InputPolicy.isAcceptablePassword(password)) {\n                // Nothing here depends on whether the account exists, so rejecting\n                // early leaks nothing - and it keeps unvalidated values out of the\n                // store lookup, the throttle key space and the log.\n                audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.INVALID_INPUT, canonicalUsername.orElse(null),\n                        canonicalSource.orElse(null), \"input rejected by policy\");\n                return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n            }\n            return attempt(canonicalUsername.get(), password, canonicalSource.get());\n        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n            // Fail closed on anything unexpected, and keep the detail out of the response.\n            audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.ERROR, null, null, \"unexpected error: \" + e.getClass().getSimpleName());\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        } finally {\n            if (password != null) {\n                Arrays.fill(password, '\\0');\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private AuthenticationResult attempt(String user, char[] password, String source) {\n        Optional<LoginThrottle.Block> block = throttle.admit(user, source);\n        if (block.isPresent()) {\n            // No key derivation here: doing the work would turn the throttle into\n            // a CPU amplifier. The decision depends on failure counts, not on\n            // whether the account exists, so it is not an existence oracle.\n            audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.THROTTLED, user, source, \"blocked by throttle: \" + block.get().name());\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n\n        Optional<CredentialRecord> record;\n        try {\n            record = credentialStore.find(user);\n        } catch (CredentialStore.CredentialStoreException | RuntimeException e) {\n            audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.ERROR, user, source, \"credential store unavailable: \" + e.getClass().getSimpleName());\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n\n        boolean verified;\n        try {\n            if (record.isEmpty()) {\n                // Equivalent work for an unknown account, so response time does\n                // not disclose which usernames exist.\n                hasher.verifyAgainstDecoy(password);\n                verified = false;\n            } else {\n                verified = hasher.verify(password, record.get());\n            }\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.ERROR, user, source, \"verification failed: \" + e.getClass().getSimpleName());\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n\n        if (!verified) {\n            throttle.recordFailure(user, source);\n            audit(record.isEmpty() ? AuthEvent.Outcome.UNKNOWN_ACCOUNT : AuthEvent.Outcome.BAD_CREDENTIALS,\n                    user, source, \"credentials did not match\");\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n\n        String sessionId;\n        try {\n            sessionId = sessions.issue(user, source);\n        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n            audit(AuthEvent.Outcome.ERROR, user, source, \"session could not be issued: \" + e.getClass().getSimpleName());\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n\n        try {\n            recordEvent(AuthEvent.Outcome.SUCCESS, user, source, \"authenticated\");\n        } catch (AuthEventLog.AuditException e) {\n            // An authentication that cannot be recorded is not granted: drop the\n            // session again and fail closed.\n            sessions.invalidate(sessionId);\n            return AuthenticationResult.failure();\n        }\n        throttle.recordSuccess(user, source);\n        return AuthenticationResult.success(user, sessionId);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates a client-presented session identifier against server-side state.\n     *\n     * <p>Shape is not proof: only a match in the registry grants anything, and a\n     * missing, unknown, malformed or expired identifier yields empty.</p>\n     */\n    public Optional<Session> resumeSession(String presentedSessionId) {\n        return sessions.validate(presentedSessionId);\n    }\n\n    /** Ends a session. Safe to call with an unknown or malformed identifier. */\n    public void signOut(String presentedSessionId) {\n        sessions.invalidate(presentedSessionId);\n    }\n\n    /** Records an event, swallowing audit failure on paths that already deny the attempt. */\n    private void audit(AuthEvent.Outcome outcome, String user, String source, String detail) {\n        try {\n            recordEvent(outcome, user, source, detail);\n        } catch (AuthEventLog.AuditException e) {\n            // The attempt is being denied regardless, so there is no decision left\n            // to fail closed on, and the detail must not reach the user.\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void recordEvent(AuthEvent.Outcome outcome, String user, String source, String detail)\n            throws AuthEventLog.AuditException {\n        String correlationId = user == null ? \"-\" : eventLog.correlationId(user);\n        eventLog.record(new AuthEvent(clock.get(), outcome,\n                LogSanitizer.maskUsername(user), correlationId, LogSanitizer.maskSource(source), detail));\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"ebd15316-6883-4a3e-a688-b2d551f14e9e","session_id":"05ae0e06-053d-4e45-86e8-cc60c97c6ae0","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"ebd15316-6883-4a3e-a688-b2d551f14e9e","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Let i < length; i++) {\n            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n            boolean alphanumeric = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9');\n            boolean separator = c == '.' || c == '_' || c == '-' || c == '@' || c == '+';\n            if (!alphanumeric && !separator) {\n                return Optional.empty();\n            }\n            if (!alphanumeric && (i == 0 || i == length - 1)) {\n                return Optional.empty();\n            }\n        }\n        // Locale.ROOT: a locale-sensitive fold would map distinct usernames\n        // together (e.g. Turkish dotless i) and let one account answer for another.\n        return Optional.of(raw.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Checks a password candidate for length only. i < part.length(); i++) {\n                char c = part.charAt(i);\n                if (c < '0' || c > '9') {\n                    return false;\n                }\n                octet = octet * 10 + (c - '0');\n            }\n            if (octet > 255) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isIpv6(String value) {\n        if (value.contains(\":::\") || value.startsWith(\":\") && !value.startsWith(\"::\")\n                || value.endsWith(\":\") && !value.endsWith(\"::\")) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        int compressions = 0;\n        for (int i = 0; i + 1 < value.length(); i++) {\n            if (value.charAt(i) == ':' && value.charAt(i + 1) == ':') {\n                compressions++;\n            }\n        }\n        if (compressions > 1) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        String[] groups = value.split(\":\", -1);\n        int present = 0;\n        for (String group : groups) {\n            if (group.isEmpty()) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (group.length() > 4) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            present++;\n        }\n        if (compressions == 0) {\n            return present == 8 && groups.length == 8;\n        }\n        return present <= 7;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates the shape of a session identifier presented by a client.\n     *\n     * <p>Shape alone proves nothing: the identifier only grants anything after\n     * {@link SessionRegistry#validate} finds it in server-side state. SessionRegistry(Duration idleTimeout, Duration absoluteTimeout, Supplier<Instant> clock) {\n        this.idleTimeout = requireBounded(\"idleTimeout\", idleTimeout);\n        this.absoluteTimeout = requireBounded(\"absoluteTimeout\", absoluteTimeout);\n        if (absoluteTimeout.compareTo(idleTimeout) < 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"absoluteTimeout must be at least Duration value) {\n        if (value == null || value.isZero() || value.isNegative() || value.compareTo(Duration.ofDays(7)) > 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(name + \" must be positive and at most 7 days\");\n        }\n        return value;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Mints a session for an already-authenticated user.\n     *\n     * @return the raw identifier, to be handed IllegalStateException(\"session capacity reached\");\n            }\n        }\n        byte[] raw = new byte[TOKEN_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(raw);\n        String identifier = encoder.encodeToString(raw);\n        java.util.Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n        sessions.put(digest(identifier), new Session(canonicalUsername, source, now, now));\n        return identifier;\n A per-account budget alone lets an\n * attacker lock a legitimate user out by failing on purpose; a per-source budget\n * alone lets a distributed attacker spread guesses across addresses. So:</p>\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the per-source budget is tight and blocks that address only;</li>\n *   <li>the per-account budget is looser, and when exhausted it only imposes a\n *       bounded cool-off that expires on its own - there is no sticky lockout and\n *       no administrative unlock, so the worst an attacker achieves is a delay,\n *       not denial of the account.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Every decision is a single atomic {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute} on the\n * counter for that key: the check and the state change that follows it cannot be\n * interleaved by a concurrent attempt, so a burst of parallel requests cannot\n * slip past the budget.</p>\n */\npublic final class LoginThrottle {\n\n    /** Hard cap on tracked keys per dimension, so attempts cannot exhaust memory. */\n    static final int MAX_TRACKED_KEYS = 50_000;\n\n    private final int sourceFailureBudget;\n    private final int accountFailureBudget;\n    private final Duration window;\n    private final Duration sourceCoolOff;\n    private final Duration accountCoolOff;\n    private final Supplier<Instant> clock;\n\n    private final Map<String, Counter> bySource = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final Map<String, Counter> byAccount = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n\n    public LoginThrottle(int sourceFailureBudget,\n                         int accountFailureBudget,\n                         Duration window,\n                         Duration sourceCoolOff,\n                         Duration accountCoolOff,\n                         Supplier<Instant> clock) {\n        this.sourceFailureBudget = InputPolicy.requireInRange(\"sourceFailureBudget\", sourceFailureBudget, 1, 1_000);\n        this.accountFailureBudget = InputPolicy.requireInRange(\"accountFailureBudget\", accountFailureBudget, 1, 10_000);\n        this.window = requirePositiveAndBounded(\"window\", window);\n        this.sourceCoolOff = requirePositiveAndBounded(\"sourceCoolOff\", sourceCoolOff);\n        this.accountCoolOff = requirePositiveAndBounded(\"accountCoolOff\", accountCoolOff);\n        this.clock = java.util.Objects.requireNonNull(clock, \"clock\");\n    }\n\n    /** Defaults: 10 failures per source and 50 per account in 15 minutes. */\n    public static LoginThrottle withDefaults() {\n        return new LoginThrottle(10, 50, Duration.ofMinutes(15), Duration.ofMinutes(15),\n                Duration.ofMinutes(5), Instant::now);\n    }\n\n    private static Duration requirePositiveAndBounded(String name, Duration value) {\n        if (value == null || value.isZero() || value.isNegative() || value.compareTo(Duration.ofDays(1)) > 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(name + \" must be positive and at most 1 day\");\n        }\n        return value;\n    }\n\n    /** Why an attempt was refused before any credential work happened. */\n    public enum Block {\n        /** This source address has spent its failure budget. */\n        SOURCE,\n        /** This account is in its bounded cool-off period. */\n        ACCOUNT,\n        /** The tracking tables are full; the attempt fails closed. */\n        CAPACITY\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically decides whether an attempt may proceed.\n     *\n     * @return empty when the attempt may proceed, otherwise the reason it may not\n     */\n    public java.util.Optional<Block> admit(String canonicalUsername, String source) {\n        Instant now = clock.get();\n        if (isBlocked(bySource, source, now)) {\n            return java.util.Optional.of(Block.SOURCE);\n        }\n        if (isBlocked(byAccount, canonicalUsername, now)) {\n            return java.util.Optional.of(Block.ACCOUNT);\n        }\n        if (atCapacity(bySource, source, now) || atCapacity(byAccount, canonicalUsername, now)) {\n            return java.util.Optional.of(Block.CAPACITY);\n        }\n        return java.util.Optional.empty();\n    }\n\n    /** Records a failed attempt against both budgets, starting a cool-off if a budget is spent. */\n    public void recordFailure(String canonicalUsername, String source) {\n        Instant now = clock.get();\n        penalise(bySource, source, now, sourceFailureBudget, sourceCoolOff);\n        penalise(byAccount, canonicalUsername, now, accountFailureBudget, accountCoolOff);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Clears the account counter after a An attacker who\n     * holds one valid account of their own would otherwise reset the per-source\n     * budget at will and guess against other accounts indefinitely from the same\n     * address. The cost is that a source which accumulated failures keeps them\n     * until the window elapses; that is bounded and self-healing, whereas the\n     * bypass would not be.</p>\n     */\n    public void recordSuccess(String canonicalUsername, String source) {\n        byAccount.remove(canonicalUsername);\n    }\n\n    private boolean isBlocked(Map<String, Counter> table, String key, Instant now) {\n        Counter[] seen = new Counter[1];\n        // One atomic compute: expire the counter or read it, never both halves\n        // of a torn view, and never a value another thread is mid-update on.\n        table.compute(key, (k, existing) -> {\n            if (existing == null || existing.isExpired(now, window)) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            seen[0] = existing;\n            return existing;\n        });\n        return seen[0] != null && seen[0].isBlocked(now);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Capacity guard. The size comparison is approximate under concurrency, which\n     * is acceptable: the bound exists to stop unbounded growth, and the slack is a\n     * few entries rather than an unbounded excess. Exceeding it denies attempts.\n     */\n    private boolean atCapacity(Map<String, Counter> table, String key, Instant now) {\n        if (table.size() < MAX_TRACKED_KEYS || table.containsKey(key)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        purgeExpired(table, now);\n        return table.size() >= MAX_TRACKED_KEYS && !table.containsKey(key);\n    }\n\n    private void purgeExpired(Map<String, Counter> table, Instant now) {\n        for (Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Counter>> it = table.entrySet().iterator(); it.hasNext(); ) {\n            Map.Entry<String, Counter> entry = it.next();\n            if (entry.getValue().isExpired(now, window)) {\n                it.remove();\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void penalise(Map<String, Counter> table, String key, Instant now, int budget, Duration coolOff) {\n        if (table.size() >= MAX_TRACKED_KEYS && !table.containsKey(key)) {\n            purgeExpired(table, now);\n            if (table.size() >= MAX_TRACKED_KEYS && !table.containsKey(key)) {\n                // The table is saturated; admit() already fails such attempts closed.\n                return;\n            }\n        }\n        table.compute(key, (k, existing) -> {\n            Counter counter = (existing == null || existing.isExpired(now, window)) ? new Counter(now) : existing;\n            return counter.withFailure(now, budget, coolOff);\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Immutable per-key counter: window start, failure count and cool-off expiry. */\n    private static final class Counter {\n        static final Counter NONE = new Counter(Instant.EPOCH);\n\n        private final Instant windowStart;\n        private final int failures;\n        private final Instant blockedUntil;\n\n        Counter(Instant windowStart) {\n            this(windowStart, 0, Instant.EPOCH);\n        }\n\n        private Counter(Instant windowStart, int failures, Instant blockedUntil) {\n            this.windowStart = windowStart;\n            this.failures = failures;\n            this.blockedUntil = blockedUntil;\n        }\n\n        boolean isBlocked(Instant now) {\n            return now.isBefore(blockedUntil);\n        }\n\n        boolean isExpired(Instant now, Duration window) {\n            return !isBlocked(now) && windowStart.plus(window).isBefore(now);\n        }\n\n now.plus(coolOff) : blockedUntil;\n            return new Counter(windowStart, next, until);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Exposed for tests only: current number of tracked source keys. */\n    int com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFileAttributeView;\nimport java.time.Duration;\nimport java.time.Instant;\nimport java.util.List;\nimport java.util.Optional;\nimport java.util.concurrent.CountDownLatch;\nimport java.util.concurrent.ExecutorService;\nimport java.util.concurrent.Executors;\nimport java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;\nimport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;\nimport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicReference;\n\n/**\n * Executable checks for the security-relevant behaviour of the component.\n *\n * <p>Plain {@code main} rather than a test framework so it runs on a bare JDK with\n * no downloaded dependencies. Run: {@code java -cp target/classes:target/test-classes\n * com.example.auth.AuthenticationSelfCheck}.</p>\n *\n * <p>The password used below is generated at runtime from {@link java.security.SecureRandom};\n * no credential is written into this source file, so nothing here works against\n * any real deployment.</p>\n */\npublic final class AuthenticationSelfCheck {\n\n    private static int failures;\n\n    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {\n        Path dir = Files.createTempDirectory(\"auth-selfcheck\");\n        Path logFile = dir.resolve(\"auth-events.log\");\n\n        char[] secret = randomPassword();\n        PasswordHasher hasher = new PasswordHasher(PasswordHasher.MIN_ITERATIONS);\n        InMemoryCredentialStore store = new InMemoryCredentialStore();\n        store.put(\"alice\", hasher.hash(secret.clone()));\n\n        AtomicReference<Instant> now = new AtomicReference<>(Instant.parse(\"2026-10-01T10:00:00Z\"));\n        LoginThrottle throttle = new LoginThrottle(5, 20, Duration.ofMinutes(15),\n                Duration.ofMinutes(15), Duration.ofMinutes(5), now::get);\n        SessionRegistry registry = new SessionRegistry(Duration.ofMinutes(30), Duration.ofHours(8), now::get);\n\n        try (FileAuthEventLog log = FileAuthEventLog.withDefaults(logFile)) {\n            AuthenticationService service =\n                    new AuthenticationService(store, hasher, throttle, registry, log, now::get);\n\n            // 1. Valid credentials authenticate and receive a fresh session identifier.\n            AuthenticationResult ok = service.authenticate(\"Alice\", secret.clone(), \"198.51.100.7\");\n            check(\"valid login succeeds\", ok.isAuthenticated());\n            String sessionId = ok.sessionId().orElse(\"\");\n            check(\"session id is well formed\", InputPolicy.isWellFormedSessionId(sessionId));\n            check(\"session resolves to server-side state\",\n                    service.resumeSession(sessionId).map(Session::username).orElse(\"\").equals(\"alice\"));\n\n            // 2. Two logins never share an identifier.\n            String second = service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"198.51.100.7\")\n                    .sessionId().orElse(\"\");\n            check(\"identifiers are unique per login\", !second.equals(sessionId));\n\n            // 3. Wrong password, unknown account and malformed input are indistinguishable.\n            AuthenticationResult wrongPassword = service.authenticate(\"alice\", randomPassword(), \"198.51.100.8\");\n            AuthenticationResult unknownUser = service.authenticate(\"nobody\", randomPassword(), \"198.51.100.8\");\n            AuthenticationResult badInput = service.authenticate(\"bad user!\", randomPassword(), \"198.51.100.8\");\n            check(\"wrong password fails\", !wrongPassword.isAuthenticated());\n            check(\"unknown account fails\", !unknownUser.isAuthenticated());\n            check(\"malformed username fails\", !badInput.isAuthenticated());\n            check(\"all failures share one message\",\n                    wrongPassword.message().equals(unknownUser.message())\n                            && unknownUser.message().equals(badInput.message())\n                            && badInput.message().equals(AuthenticationResult.GENERIC_FAILURE_MESSAGE));\n            check(\"failures carry no session\", wrongPassword.sessionId().isEmpty()\n                    && unknownUser.sessionId().isEmpty() && badInput.sessionId().isEmpty());\n\n            // 4. Per-source budget blocks that source but leaves the account usable\n            //    from elsewhere - an attacker cannot lock the legitimate user out.\n            for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {\n                service.authenticate(\"alice\", randomPassword(), \"203.0.113.9\");\n            }\n            check(\"exhausted source is blocked\",\n                    !service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"203.0.113.9\").isAuthenticated());\n            check(\"legitimate user still signs in from another source\",\n                    service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"198.51.100.7\").isAuthenticated());\n            check(\"a success elsewhere does not reset the blocked source's budget\",\n                    !service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"203.0.113.9\").isAuthenticated());\n\n            // 5. The account cool-off expires on its own; no unlock step is needed.\n            for (int i = 0; i < 20; i++) {\n                service.authenticate(\"alice\", randomPassword(), \"203.0.113.\" + (10 + i % 200));\n            }\n            check(\"account cool-off engages\",\n                    !service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"192.0.2.5\").isAuthenticated());\n            now.set(now.get().plus(Duration.ofMinutes(6)));\n            check(\"account cool-off expires unattended\",\n                    service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"192.0.2.5\").isAuthenticated());\n\n            // 6. Session identifiers from the caller are never honoured.\n            check(\"forged identifier rejected\", service.resumeSession(\"A\".repeat(43)).isEmpty());\n            check(\"empty identifier rejected\", service.resumeSession(\"\").isEmpty());\n            check(\"null identifier rejected\", service.resumeSession(null).isEmpty());\n\n            // 7. Timeouts and sign-out are enforced server-side.\n            String live = service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"192.0.2.5\").sessionId().orElseThrow();\n            service.signOut(live);\n            check(\"sign-out invalidates\", service.resumeSession(live).isEmpty());\n            String idle = service.authenticate(\"alice\", secret.clone(), \"192.0.2.5\").sessionId().orElseThrow();\n            now.set(now.get().plus(Duration.ofMinutes(31)));\n            check(\"idle session expires\", service.resumeSession(idle).isEmpty());\n\n            // 8. Concurrent attempts cannot exceed the budget.\n            checkConcurrentBudget(store, hasher, registry, log, now::get);\n        }\n\n        // 9. The log is private, holds no secrets, and resists log forging.\n        List<String> lines = Files.readAllLines(logFile);\n        check(\"events were retained\", lines.size() >= 10);\n        String joined = String.join(\"\\n\", lines);\n        check(\"password never appears in the log\", !joined.contains(new String(secret)));\n        check(\"full username never appears in the log\", !joined.contains(\"\\\"alice\\\"\"));\n        check(\"full source address never appears in the log\", !joined.contains(\"198.51.100.7\"));\n        check(\"source is masked to a prefix\", joined.contains(\"198.51.100.0/24\"));\n        check(\"one record per attempt\", lines.stream().allMatch(l -> l.startsWith(\"ts=\\\"\")));\n\n        PosixFileAttributeView view = Files.getFileAttributeView(logFile, PosixFileAttributeView.class,\n                LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        check(\"log file is owner-only\", view == null || view.readAttributes().permissions().equals(\n                java.util.EnumSet.of(java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ,\n                        java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE)));\n\n        // 10. If an attacker makes 10 failed attempts from source X, source X blocked. Fine.\n\nHowever — the per-account budget: 50 failures/15min → 5 min cool-off. After cool-off expires, isExpired checks windowStart+15min < now. So the counter persists. Each new failure after that: failures keeps incrementing, next>=budget → sets blockedUntil = now+5min again. Let's re-examine the concurrency/rate-limit bypass (item 7) — I think that's the strongest: High.\n\nLet me also reconsider: does `admit()` really allow unbounded parallel guesses? Yes. Sequence per request: admit() (read-only), then PBKDF2 verify (~100ms at 210k iters), then recordFailure. So the number of guesses admitted concurrently from a single source = number of concurrent requests the server accepts. With 200 threads for 15 minutes, an attacker gets a huge number of guesses per IP: each \"wave\" of concurrent requests evaluates all of them. So effective limit per source ≈ (concurrency) × (15min / PBKDF2_time) — vastly more than 10. This defeats the advertised control. Definitely High.\n\nAlso: because `admit` never reserves, an attacker doesn't even need much concurrency — they can pipeline: the block only applies after failures are *recorded*, and 10 failures only accrue serially. With C concurrent connections you get ~C guesses per round-trip. And the account budget of 50 similarly becomes 50 + concurrency per round → attacker can also trivially trigger the account cool-off (DoS, item 4). Both stem from the same root cause: no reservation/serialization across the check→act gap. Our code: default 210,000 (fine per OWASP 2023 for SHA-512), but MIN_ITERATIONS = 100,000 is accepted both for the hasher config AND for stored CredentialRecords. A deployment can legitimately construct `new PasswordHasher(100_000)` — the self-check itself does this. So the KDF floor is below the OWASP recommended 210,000 for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512. This is a Low/Medium \"weak KDF parameter accepted\" finding (CWE-916: Use of Password Hash With Insufficient Computational Effort). The library's weakness table calls \"Low iteration PBKDF2\" High risk; but here the *default* is fine and only the accepted floor is low, and the docs cite OWASP's 210k floor while permitting half of it. I'll grade Medium (config-accepted weakness) — actually it's the minimum accepted, requires misconfiguration. I'll grade Low? Hmm. Because `CredentialRecord` also accepts 100,000-iteration records, a store that silently holds 100k records is accepted, and the *decoy* uses the configured count → timing divergence. I'll report as Low-Medium: Medium feels right given CWE-916 and that the component's own validation permits it while claiming an OWASP floor. I'll say Medium... Let me weigh: the primary control (default) is correct; exploitation requires a deployer choosing 100k or a store with weaker records. Medium severity is defensible; I'll use Low? I'll go with Medium for the KDF floor since CWE-916 and the explicit contradiction with the stated OWASP floor. Hmm, but honestly, 100,000 iterations of PBKDF2-SHA512 is still reasonably strong (OWASP 2023 floor for PBKDF2-SHA256 is 600,000, for SHA-512 is 210,000; older guidance was 310k for SHA-256... ). 100k is below all current floors. Medium.\n\nAlso the MAX_ITERATIONS = 10,000,000 accepted from a store-supplied record → CPU DoS amplifier per login if the store is compromised/untrusted (README explicitly says the store is state \"this component does not own\", i.e., not trusted). Each accepted record with 10M iterations costs ~50x the default per login. And worse — the timing oracle: known vs unknown account use different iteration counts. I'll fold the iteration-range concern into one finding about trusting store-supplied work factor (CWE-400 / CWE-410 insufficient resource pool? Better CWE-400 uncontrolled resource consumption).
